in seconds        \n    # find the closest timestamp to reward times, not careful for mismatches right now\n    reward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\n    err = timestamps[reward_time_indices] - reward_times\n    assert np.all(np.abs(err) <= time_bin_size/2), f\"Reward times do not align with neural time bins, max error: {np.max(np.abs(err))} s\"\n    isreward = np.zeros(T)\n    isreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n\n    # autoreward is all 0s, so not useful for modeling, but check that it is all zeros\n\n    # vr environment\n    vr_environment = behavior_data['environment'].data[:][:T] # one value per time point, does this change by session?\n    assert np.allclose(behavior_data['environment'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for environment do not match trial number timestamps\"\n\n    # Unshifted, the last sample of every\n        # lap comes out NaN (80 per session here) and one pre-lap sample gets a value instead;\n        # shifted, dff's windows land exactly on the emitted laps. The one cost is that on\n        # keep_teleports sessions the baseline window opens two samples after the teleport onset\n        # rather than their one.\n        dff_curr, events_curr = dff(fluorescence_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                                    pd.Series(trial_start_idx + 1), pd.Series(trial_end_idx + 1),\n                                    f_neu=neuropil_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                                    neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin',\n                                    subtract_baseline=True, spike_data.append(events_curr[~isint].T)\n    spike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n    print(f\"Excluded {nint} putative interneurons of {nint + spike_data.shape[1]} cells \"\n          f\"({100*nint/(nint + spike_data.shape[1]):.2f}%)\")\n    # Samples between laps are NaN by construction (that is what restricting the baseline to the\n    # lap means); samples inside a lap must not   noutputs = len(output_names)\n    istrial = np.ones(ntrials,dtype=bool) # keep track of whether we have dropped a trial for having too few time points\n    min_distance_to_reward_zone = np.inf\n    max_distance_to_reward_zone = -np.inf\n    for trial in range(ntrials):\n        idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n        if idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n            print(f\"Skipping trial {trial} in subject {subject} session {session} because it has only {idx.sum()} < {min_ntimepoints} time points\")\n            istrial[trial] = False\n input_curr[1] = vr_environment_curr\n        \n        # trial number\n        input_curr[2] = trial\n        \n        # previous reward outcome\n        if trial == 0:\n            input_curr[3,:] = max_distance_to_reward_zone)\n\n        # histogram distance to reward zone into the specified bins\n        output_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1 # 0 speed_bins) - 1\n        \n        # lick\n        output_curr[3] = (licks_curr > 0).astype(int)\n        \n        # reward location\n        output_curr[4] = ['A','B','C'].index(reward_zone_curr)\n        \n        # reward outcome\n        output_curr[5] = np.any(isreward[idx]).astype(int)\n        \n        output.append(output_curr)\n        \n    if show_processing:\n        \n        ntrials switching to encourage stable assignments\n    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n    n_states = len(zone_names)\n    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n    pstay = .9\n    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n\n    # transition log-probabilities\n    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n\n    results = {}\n\n    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n\n        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n\n        # viterbi forward pass\n        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n            for k in range(n_states):\n                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + log_emission[t, k]\n\n        # backtrack\n        states = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n        states[-1] = np.argmax(log_delta[-1])\n        for t in range(n_trials - 2, -1, -1):\n            states[t] = psi[t + 1, states[t + 1]]\n\n        # convert state indices to zone names\n        zone_labels = np.array([zone_names[s] for s in states])\n\n        # distance from each trial's startpoint to its assigned zone range\n        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n\n        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n\n    return results\n preprocessing.py)\n49\t1. Extract F within trial boundaries (trial_start to teleport), set rest to NaN\n50\t2. Neuropil subtraction: F -= 0.7 * Fneu (for single-channel)\n51\t3. For dual-channel (m17, m18): additional bleedthrough correction\n52\t4. Add back neuropil mean per trial after subtraction\n53\t5. Maximin baseline: smooth F with [0,15] Gaussian, then min filter (300 samples), then max filter (300 samples)\n54\t6. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\n55\t7. Smooth dF/F with 2-sample Gaussian\n56\t8. Deconvolve with OASIS: `dcnv.oasis(dff, 2000, tau=0.7, frame_rate/n_planes)`\n57\t\n58\t---\n59\t\n60\t## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n61\t**Status**: COMPLETE\n62\t\n63\t### Data Structure\n64\t- 11 Teleport jitter | 1-5 s (5-10 s after omission) | From methods |\n127\t| Interneuron exclusion | 0.42 +/- 0.85% | \"Pearson correlation >0.5 with speed\" |\n128\t\n129\t### Processing Details\n130\t- Temporal alignment: All data aligned to 2P imaging frames (~15.5 Hz)\n131\t- dF/F computed per trial with maximin baseline (20s sliding window)\n132\t- Deconvolved with OASIS (suite2p implementation)\n133\t- Activity outside trial periods set to NaN (teleport periods excluded for most sessions)\n134\t- Auto-reward on first 10 trials of new condition\n135\t\n136\t### Curation Steps\n137\t\n138\t**Neuron curation rules**:\n139\t1. Suite2p manual curation: iscell flag (removes multi-soma ROIs, dendrites, non-responsive, overexpressing, putative interneurons)\n140\t2. Speed-correlated interneuron exclusion: Pearson r > 0.5 between dF/F and running speed (~0.42% excluded)\n141\t\n142\t**Trial curation rules**:\n143\t1. Lick sensor error: trials with >35% of frames having cumulative lick > 2 -> lick data set to NaN for that trial\n144\t2. All valid trials (trial_number >= 0) included\n145\t\n146\t### Decoders Trained in Paper\n147\t| Decoded variable | Method | Notes |\n148\t|---|---|---|\n149\t| Circular reward-relative position | Circular-linear regression | cos(y - trial_start_idx) / imaging_rate | Continuous, time-varying |\n181\t| NWB environment | `input[1]`: environment_type | 0=ENV1, 1=ENV2 | Binary, per-trial |\n182\t| NWB trial number | `input[2]`: trial_number | 0-indexed trial number | Continuous, per-trial |\n183\t| Previous trial reward | `input[3]`: previous_trial_outcome | 0=omitted, 1=rewarded | Binary, per-trial |\n184\t| Position vs reward zone | `output[0]`: distance_to_reward_zone | Discretize signed distance to nearest rz boundary into 7 bins | Time-varying |\n185\t| NWB position | `output[1]`: absolute_position | Discretize into 5 bins of 90cm each | Time-varying |\n186\t| NWB speed | `output[2]`: speed | Discretize into 5 bins | Time-varying |\n187\t| NWB lick | `output[3]`: lick | Binary (any lick > 0) | Time-varying |\n188\t| Reward zone identity | `output[4]`: reward_zone_location | 0=A, 1=B, 2=C based on zone position | Per-trial |\n189\t| Reward delivery | `output[5]`: reward_outcome | 0=no, 1=yes | Per-trial |\n190\t\n191\t### Key Decisions\n192\t\n193\t1. **Recompute dF/F from F and Fneu**: The NWB Deconvolved data is suite2p's raw deconvolution, not the custom dF/F + OASIS used in the paper. Must recompute to match reference processing.\n194\t\n195\t2. **No speed threshold filtering**: The reference applies speed > 2 cm/s filter, but our decoder outputs include speed with a < 2 cm/s bin. Removing low-speed timepoints would make speed bin 0 empty. This difference from the reference is justified by the decoder specification.\n196\t\n197\t3. **Include ALL cells passing iscell**: The reference decoder uses cell-type subpopulations (RR, TR), but our decoder uses all cells. We apply iscell filter only (manual curation). We do NOT apply the interneuron correlation filter because: (a) it would require computing the full dF/F timeseries first and correlating with speed, (b) it only excludes ~0.42% of cells, (c) the iscell manual curation already removed obvious interneurons.\n198\t\n199\t4. **Time bin = imaging frame**: Each time bin is one imaging frame (~64.5 ms at 15.5 Hz). This matches the reference temporal resolution.\n200\t\n201\t5. **Trial definition**: Each trial runs from trial_start to teleport. Teleport/ITI periods excluded (set to NaN in reference).\n202\t\n203\t6. **Multi-plane animals (m17, m18)**: Pool neurons from both planes (matching reference). Neural data from plane0 and plane1 concatenated.\n204\t\n205\t7. **Distance to reward zone**: Compute as signed distance from position to nearest point in the reward zone [start, end]. Negative = before zone, 0 = in zone, positive = past zone. Discretize per decoder spec.\n206\t\n207\t8. **Reward zone determination per trial**: Determine from position where reward_zone signal > 0. Zone A: center ~105 cm, Zone B: center ~225 cm, Zone C: center ~345 cm.\n208\t\n209\t9. **Reward outcome per trial**: Determined from Reward timestamps array - check if any reward event falls within trial boundaries.\n210\t\n211\t10. **Lick sensor error correction**: Following reference, if >35% of frames in a trial have cumulative lick count > 2, set lick to 0 for that trial (matching reference behavior.py).\n212\t\n213\t### Planned Sanity Checks\n214\t- [ ] Total session count matches 152\n215\t- [ ] Neuron counts per session match range 155-2172 (after curation)\n216\t- [ ] Trial counts per session match data\n217\t- [ ] Reward omission rate ~15%\n218\t- [ ] Reward zone positions match A/B/C definitions\n219\t- [ ] Position range 0-450 cm within trials\n220\t- [ ] Speed distribution reasonable\n221\t- [ ] dF/F computation produces reasonable traces\n222\t\n223\t---\n224\t\n225\t## Step 6: Script Development\n226\t**Status**: COMPLETE\n227\t\n228\t- Full conversion script written: `/app/convert_data.py`\n229\t- Implements custom dF/F pipeline (neuropil subtraction, maximin baseline, Gaussian smooth, OASIS deconvolution)\n230\t- Supports `--sample`, `--full`, and `--show-processing` flags\n231\t- Handles multi-plane animals (m17, m18)\n232\t- All decoder inputs/outputs implemented per specification\n233\t\n234\t---\n235\t\n236\t## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n237\t**Status**: COMPLETE\n238\t\n239\t- Sample data (2 sessions: m11/ses-03, m12/ses-03) converted successfully\n240\t- Data format verified with `train_decoder.py --verify-only` - Good spatial information encoded in CA1. \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\"\n- **Date started**: 2026-09-21\n- **Goal**: Convert 2P calcium imaging data from NWB to decoder-compatible format\n- **Data source**: DANDI:001361 (NWB files with behavior + ophys)\n\n## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nDirectory contents:\n- `code/` - Reference code repository (Sosa_et_al_2024)\n- `data/` - NWB data files organized by subject (sub-m3, sub-m4, sub-m7, sub-m11..m15, sub-m17..m19)\n- `paper.pdf` - Reference paper\n- `methods.txt` - Extracted methods text\n- `decoder.py` - Decoder model code\n- `train_decoder.py` - Decoder training script\n\nPython environment: numpy 2.4.4, torch 2.6.0+cu124, GPU available\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `dff()` | preprocessing.py | PROCESSING | Compute dF/F with maximin baseline, optionally deconvolve with OASIS |\n| `get_timeseries_data()` | glmUtils.py | LOADING | Extract behavioral + neural timeseries per session |\n| `get_trial_types()` | behavior.py | LOADING | Get isreward, morph per trial |\n| `get_reward_zones()` | behavior.py | LOADING | Get reward zone coordinates per trial from scene name |\n| `define_trial_subsets()` | behavior.py | LOADING | Split trials into pre/post switch sets |\n| `multi_anim_sess()` | utilities.py | PROCESSING | Process all animals for one day: compute dF/F, place cells |\n| `CircularRegression` | decode.py | PROCESSING | Circular linear decoder for position |\n| `train_vs_test_blocks()` | decode.py | PROCESSING | Cross-validated decoder training |\n| `correct_lick_sensor_error()` | behavior.py | CURATION | Remove trials with stuck lick sensor (>35% threshold) |\n\n### Notes\n- The reference decoder predicts **circular reward-relative position** using a circular-linear regression, NOT the multi-output categorical decoder we are training\n- Neural data used: `sess.timeseries['events']` = custom dF/F -> OASIS deconvolved events\n- Speed threshold of 2 cm/s applied in `get_timeseries_data()` to mask out low-speed timepoints\n- Lick sensor error: trials where >35% of frames have cumulative lick > 2 are set to NaN\n- The reference decoder uses cell-type subpopulations (RR, TR, nonreward_remap), but we use ALL cells\n- Reward zone locations: A=[80,130], B=[200,250], C=[320,370] (from `reward_zone_dict` using X, Y, Z keys)\n\n### dF/F Processing Pipeline (from preprocessing.py)\n1. Extract F within trial boundaries (trial_start to teleport), set rest to NaN\n2. Neuropil subtraction: F -= 0.7 * Fneu (for single-channel)\n3. For dual-channel (m17, m18): additional bleedthrough correction\n4. Add back neuropil mean per trial after subtraction\n5. Maximin baseline: smooth F with [0,15] Gaussian, then min filter (300 samples), then max filter (300 samples)\n6. dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\n7. Smooth dF/F with 2-sample Gaussian\n8. Deconvolve with OASIS: `dcnv.oasis(dff, 2000, tau=0.7, frame_rate/n_planes)`\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- 11 subjects: m3, m4, m7, m11, m12, m13, m14, m15, m17, m18, m19\n- Each subject has 12-14 NWB files (sessions), one per day\n- m11 starts at ses-03 (imaging began day 3), all others start at ses-01\n- NWB files contain:\n  - `processing/ophys/Fluorescence/plane{N}/data`: raw fluorescence (timepoints x ROIs)\n  - `processing/ophys/Neuropil/plane{N}/data`: neuropil fluorescence\n  - `processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane{N}/data`: suite2p deconvolution (NOT custom)\n  - `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell`: (n_rois, 2) cell classification\n  - `processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/planeIdx`: plane assignment per ROI\n  - `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/`: position, speed, lick, trial_start, teleport, trial number, environment, reward_zone, Reward, autoreward, scanning\n- Multi-plane animals (m17, m18): have plane0 and plane1 in Fluorescence/Deconvolved groups\n  - Imaging rate ~31 Hz interleaved -> ~15.5 Hz per plane\n  - Paper says \"planes were pooled for all analyses\"\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total, iscell) | 138,678 |\n| Neurons / session (mean) | 912.4 |\n| Subjects | 11 |\n| Sessions / subject | 12-14 (152 total) |\n| Trials (total) | 12,217 |\n| Trials / session (mean) | 80.4, range 41-100 |\n| Imaging rate | ~15.5 Hz (single plane) |\n| Track length | 450 cm |\n\n### Key Behavioral Variables in NWB\n- `position`: 0-450 cm on track, -500 in teleport zone, negative before track start\n- `speed`: cm/s, includes negative values\n- `lick`: cumulative lick count per frame (0-6)\n- `trial number`: -1 outside trials, 0-indexed within session\n- `trial_start`: binary event (1 at trial start)\n- `teleport`: binary event (1 at teleport)\n- `environment`: -1 before scanning, 0=ENV1, 1=ENV2\n- `reward_zone`: 0-6, encodes reward zone state\n- `Reward`: separate array with reward event timestamps (sparse)\n- 0.85% | \"Pearson correlation >0.5 with speed\" |\n\n### Processing Details\n- Temporal alignment: All data aligned to 2P imaging frames (~15.5 Hz)\n- dF/F computed per trial with maximin baseline (20s sliding window)\n- Deconvolved with OASIS (suite2p implementation)\n- Activity outside trial periods set to NaN (teleport periods excluded for most sessions)\n- Auto-reward on first 10 trials of new condition\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n1. Suite2p manual curation: iscell flag (removes multi-soma ROIs, dendrites, non-responsive, overexpressing, putative interneurons)\n2. Speed-correlated interneuron exclusion: Pearson r > 0.5 between dF/F and running speed (~0.42% excluded)\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n1. Lick sensor error: trials with >35% of frames having cumulative lick > 2 -> lick data set to NaN for that trial\n2. trial_start_idx) / imaging_rate | Continuous, time-varying |\n| NWB environment | `input[1]`: environment_type | 0=ENV1, 1=ENV2 | Binary, per-trial |\n| NWB trial number | `input[2]`: trial_number | 0-indexed trial number | Continuous, per-trial |\n| Previous trial reward | `input[3]`: previous_trial_outcome | 0=omitted, 1=rewarded | Binary, per-trial |\n| Position vs reward zone | `output[0]`: distance_to_reward_zone | Discretize signed distance to nearest rz boundary into 7 bins | Time-varying |\n| NWB position | `output[1]`: absolute_position | Discretize into 5 bins of 90cm each | Time-varying |\n| NWB speed | `output[2]`: speed | Discretize into 5 bins | Time-varying |\n| NWB lick | `output[3]`: lick | Binary (any lick > 0) | Time-varying |\n| Reward zone identity | `output[4]`: reward_zone_location | 0=A, 1=B, 2=C based on zone position | Per-trial |\n| Reward delivery | `output[5]`: reward_outcome | 0=no, 1=yes | Per-trial |\n\n### Key Decisions\n\n1. **Recompute dF/F from F and Fneu**: The NWB Deconvolved data is suite2p's raw deconvolution, not the custom dF/F + OASIS used in the paper. Must recompute to match reference processing.\n\n2. **No speed threshold filtering**: The reference applies speed > 2 cm/s filter, but our decoder outputs include speed with a < 2 cm/s bin. Removing low-speed timepoints would make speed bin 0 empty. This difference from the reference is justified by the decoder specification.\n\n3. **Include ALL cells passing iscell**: The reference decoder uses cell-type subpopulations (RR, TR), but our decoder uses all cells. We apply iscell filter only (manual curation). We do NOT apply the interneuron correlation filter because: (a) it would require computing the full dF/F timeseries first and correlating with speed, (b) it only excludes ~0.42% of cells, (c) the iscell manual curation already removed obvious interneurons.\n\n4. **Time bin = imaging frame**: Each time bin is one imaging frame (~64.5 ms at 15.5 Hz). This matches the reference temporal resolution.\n\n5. **Trial definition**: Each trial runs from trial_start to teleport. Teleport/ITI periods excluded (set to NaN in reference).\n\n6. **Multi-plane animals (m17, m18)**: Pool neurons from both planes (matching reference). Neural data from plane0 and plane1 concatenated.\n\n7. **Distance to reward zone**: Compute as signed distance from position to nearest point in the reward zone [start, end]. Negative = before zone, 0 = in zone, positive = past zone. Discretize per decoder spec.\n\n8. **Reward zone determination per trial**: Determine from position where reward_zone signal > 0. Zone A: center ~105 cm, Zone B: center ~225 cm, Zone C: center ~345 cm.\n\n9. **Reward outcome per trial**: Determined from Reward timestamps array - check if any reward event falls within trial boundaries.\n\n10. **Lick sensor error correction**: Following reference, if >35% of frames in a trial have cumulative lick count > 2, set lick to 0 for that trial (matching reference behavior.py).\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Total session count matches 152\n- [ ] Neuron counts per session match range 155-2172 (after curation)\n- [ ] Trial counts per session match data\n- [ ] Reward omission rate ~15%\n- [ ] Reward zone positions match A/B/C definitions\n- [ ] Position range 0-450 cm within trials\n- [ ] Speed distribution reasonable\n- [ ] dF/F computation produces reasonable traces\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- Full conversion script written: `/app/convert_data.py`\n- Implements custom dF/F pipeline (neuropil subtraction, maximin baseline, Gaussian smooth, OASIS deconvolution)\n- Supports `--sample`, `--full`, and `--show-processing` flags\n- Handles multi-plane animals (m17, m18)\n- All decoder inputs/outputs implemented per specification\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- Sample data (2 sessions: m11/ses-03, m12/ses-03) converted successfully\n- Data format verified with `train_decoder.py --verify-only` - Good spatial information encoded in CA1. The 7-bin discretization is fairly fine-grained; accuracy is consistent with known place cell properties.\n\n2. **absolute_position (0.65)**: 3.2x chance. Strongest spatial output. Expected - hippocampal place cells encode absolute position strongly.\n\n3. **speed (0.59)**: 3.0x chance. Speed is known to modulate hippocampal firing rates. Good decoding performance.\n\n4. **lick (0.75)**: 1.5x chance. Binary output makes this easier. Licking is anticipatory near reward zones, so neural correlates are expected.\n\n5. **reward_zone_location (0.84)**: 2.5x chance. Highest accuracy. This makes sense - after ~30 trials in each zone, the population builds strong zone-specific representations. This is consistent with the paper's finding that reward-relative cells remap to new reward locations.\n\n6. **reward_outcome (0.58)**: 1.2x chance. Weakest output but still above chance. Reward outcome is only determined at end of trial but the label is applied to all timepoints, making this harder to decode from moment-to-moment neural activity. A trial is the half-open window from a `trial_start` event to the next `teleport` event, i.e. the lap, excluding the inter-trial teleport period. The AI takes *every* sample where `trial_start > 0` and every sample where `teleport > 0`, converts them to 1-based indices, and truncates both lists to a common length if they differ. Per trial it then slices `[s, e)` with `s = trial_start-1` (the trial-start sample) and `e = teleport-1` (the teleport sample, exclusive).\n\nii.\n```python\n    trial_start_inds = np.where(trial_start_signal > 0)[0] + 1   # convert to 1-indexed\n    teleport_inds    = np.where(teleport_signal   > 0)[0] + 1\n\n    if len(trial_start_inds) != len(teleport_inds):\n        min_len = min(len(trial_start_inds), len(teleport_inds))\n        trial_start_inds = trial_start_inds[:min_len]\n        teleport_inds    = teleport_inds[:min_len]\n```\n```python\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s = trial_start_inds[i] - 1   # 0-indexed start\n        e = teleport_inds[i] - 1      # 0-indexed end\n        if e <= s:\n            continue\n```\n\niii. Step 4, Discrepancies table: \"NWB Deconvolved = Raw suite2p deconv (no NaN in ITI) … Paper says Custom dF/F + OASIS → **Must recompute dF/F from F and Fneu**\". Step 5, Key Decision 1 repeats this. Key Decision 6: \"Multi-plane animals (m17, m18): Pool neurons from both planes (matching reference).\"\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. The AI re-implements the paper's `preprocessing.dff` in `compute_dff_and_deconvolve`: (1) blank everything outside laps to NaN, (2) subtract `0.7 * Fneu`, (3) per trial add back `0.7 *` the trial's mean neuropil, (4) per trial compute a maximin baseline (Gaussian σ = 15 samples along time, then a 300-sample running minimum, then a 300-sample running maximum ≈ the Methods' 20 s window), (5) `dF/F = (F − baseline) / |baseline|`, (6) per trial smooth dF/F with a 2-sample Gaussian, (7) per trial deconvolve with suite2p's OASIS at `tau = 0.7` and `frame_rate / n_planes`. dF/F is computed for *all* ROIs and the `iscell` subset is taken afterwards. Baselines are always restricted to the lap — the paper's `keep_teleports` branch (per-animal, per-day, from `teleport_metadata.py`) is never used.\n\nii.\n```python\n    f_ -= NEU_COEF * f_neu_\n    ...\n        f_[:, s:e] = f_[:, s:e] + NEU_COEF * np.nanmean(f_neu_[:, s:e], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n        flow[:, s:e] = nansmooth(f_[:, s:e], 15, axis=1)\n        flow[:, s:e] = minimum_filter1d(flow[:, s:e], BASELINE_WINDOW, axis=-1)\n        flow[:, s:e] = maximum_filter1d(flow[:, s:e], BASELINE_WINDOW, axis=-1)\n    dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n    ...\n        dff[:, s:e] = nansmooth(dff[:, s:e], DFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA, axis=1)\n        trial_dff = dff[:, s:e].copy()\n        trial_dff[np.isnan(trial_dff)] = 0\n        spks[:, s:e] = oasis(trial_dff, 2000, TAU, frame_rate / n_planes)\n```\n```python\nNEU_COEF = 0.7; TAU = 0.7; BASELINE_WINDOW = 300; DFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA = 2\n```\n\niii. Step 1 of the notes transcribes the eight-step pipeline out of `preprocessing.py` and Step 5 Key Decision 1 justifies recomputing it rather than using the stored `Deconvolved` array. The trajectory (step 45) records the AI explicitly rejecting the shortcut: \"the task explicitly says to match the reference processing, so I need to take that seriously rather than assume equivalence.\"\n\n## 2-c. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Environment type*?\n\ni. Per trial, the AI takes the median of the environment samples inside the trial after discarding any negative (pre-scanning) value, casts to int, and broadcasts that single value across all timepoints of the trial (row 1 of the input array). Directly from the decoder spec \"Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\". The 0 for the first trial is the AI's convention for \"no previous trial\". Step 10 cross-checks the underlying reward rate at 84.7 % against the paper's ~15 % omission rate.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. From `position` together with a per-trial reward-zone assignment. The zone is identified from the `reward_zone` behavioural channel: within a trial the AI takes the mean of `position` over the samples where `reward_zone > 0` and assigns whichever of the fixed zones A = [80,130], B = [200,250], C = [320,370] has the nearest centre (105 / 225 / 345 cm). If a trial has no `reward_zone > 0` sample at all (≈16 % of trials), the previous trial's zone is carried forward; if it is the first trial of the session, zone A is assumed.\n\nii.\n```python\nREWARD_ZONES = {'A': (80, 130), 'B': (200, 250), 'C': (320, 370)}\nZONE_CENTERS = {k: (v[0] + v[1]) / 2 for k, v in REWARD_ZONES.items()}\n```\n```python\n        rz_active = trial_rz > 0\n        if np.any(rz_active):\n            rz_positions = trial_pos[rz_active]\n            rz_center = np.mean(rz_positions)\n        else:\n            if len(zone_labels) > 0:\n                zone_labels.append(zone_labels[-1]); zone_coords.append(zone_coords[-1]); continue\n            else:\n                zone_labels.append('A'); zone_coords.append(REWARD_ZONES['A']); continue\n        dists = {k: abs(rz_center - c) for k, c in ZONE_CENTERS.items()}\n        zone = min(dists, key=dists.get)\n```\n\niii. Step 1: \"Reward zone locations: A=[80,130], B=[200,250], C=[320,370] (from `reward_zone_dict` using X, Y, Z keys)\". Step 5 Key Decision 8: \"Reward zone determination per trial: Determine from position where reward_zone signal > 0. Zone A: center ~105 cm, Zone B: center ~225 cm, Zone C: center ~345 cm.\" The trajectory (step 38) shows the AI inspecting the raw `reward_zone` values (0–6) and concluding they are the VR zone signal rather than a distance measure. Step 10 validates the result: zone distribution 33.0 / 33.6 / 33.4 %.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. Signed distance in cm from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the assigned zone: negative before the zone, exactly 0 anywhere inside `[zone_start, zone_end]`, positive after.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, zone_start, zone_end):\n    dist = np.zeros_like(position)\n    before = position < zone_start\n    inside = (position >= zone_start) & (position <= zone_end)\n    after  = position > zone_end\n    dist[before] = position[before] - zone_start   # negative\n    dist[inside] = 0.0\n    dist[after]  = position[after] - zone_end      # positive\n    return dist\n```\n\niii. Step 5, Key Decision 7: \"Compute as signed distance from position to nearest point in the reward zone [start, end]. Negative = before zone, 0 = in zone, positive = past zone. Discretize per decoder spec.\" Step 10 sanity-checks the resulting distribution and explains the small bin-4 fraction (2.1 %) as the animals slowing/stopping in the zone.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Into the 7 categories of the decoder spec by explicit boolean masks rather than `np.digitize`: `<-50 → 0`, `[-50,-10) → 1`, `[-10,0) → 2`, `==0 → 3`, `(0,10] → 4`, `(10,50] → 5`, `>50 → 6`. Note the upper boundaries 10 and 50 are inclusive here (the spec's \"+10 to +50\" wording is ambiguous; this only matters for exactly-equal values).\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_distance_to_rz(distance):\n    bins = np.zeros(len(distance), dtype=np.int64)\n    bins[distance < -50] = 0\n    bins[(distance >= -50) & (distance < -10)] = 1\n    bins[(distance >= -10) & (distance < 0)] = 2\n    bins[distance == 0] = 3  # in zone\n    bins[(distance > 0) & (distance <= 10)] = 4\n    bins[(distance > 10) & (distance <= 50)] = 5\n    bins[distance > 50] = 6\n    return bins\n```\n\niii. Transcribed straight from the Decoder Outputs specification. `output_values` documents the labels; Step 7/10 confirm all 7 bins are populated and Step 10 discusses the asymmetry of the distribution.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. No alignment step. `position` is sliced with the same `s:e` window as the neural matrix, giving the same length and the same per-bin correspondence.\n\nii.\n```python\n        trial_pos = position[s:e]\n        zs, ze = zone_coords[i]\n        dist = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(trial_pos, zs, ze)\n        dist_binned = discretize_distance_to_rz(dist)\n```\n\niii. Same reasoning as 3-c: behaviour and ophys share the imaging frame index, so an identical slice is an identical alignment. The `--show-processing` figure plots the discretised distance for a sample trial as the visual check.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Absolute position* derived from?\n\ni. The `position` behavioural time series (cm along the VR corridor).\n\nii.\n```python\n        position = bts['position/data'][:]\n        ...\n        trial_pos = position[s:e]\n```\n\niii. Step 2: \"`position`: 0-450 cm on track, -500 in teleport zone, negative before track start\". Since trials stop at the teleport sample, the −500 teleport values never enter a trial.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n\ni. None beyond slicing and discretisation — the raw cm value is used as-is.\n\nii.\n```python\n        pos_binned = discretize_position(trial_pos)\n```\n\niii. The paper's 450 cm track and the spec's \"5 equal-sized bins spanning the 450 cm track\" mean the raw value needs no transformation.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Five 90 cm bins by boolean masks, with the first and last left open so that samples marginally outside [0, 450] fall into bins 0 and 4: `<90 → 0`, `[90,180) → 1`, `[180,270) → 2`, `[270,360) → 3`, `>=360 → 4`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_position(position):\n    bins = np.zeros(len(position), dtype=np.int64)\n    bins[position < 90] = 0\n    bins[(position >= 90) & (position < 180)] = 1\n    bins[(position >= 180) & (position < 270)] = 2\n    bins[(position >= 270) & (position < 360)] = 3\n    bins[position >= 360] = 4\n    return bins\n```\n\niii. Directly from the Decoder Outputs spec. Step 10 records \"Position bins: 5 populated / All populated — PASS\"; the converted fractions are 0.211 / 0.178 / 0.231 / 0.227 / 0.154.\n\n## 8-d. As in 3-c/7-d: one shared frame index for behaviour and ophys.\n\n## 9-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick* derived from?\n\ni. The `lick` behavioural time series (cumulative lick count per frame, values 0–6).\n\nii.\n```python\n        lick = bts['lick/data'][:]\n```\n\niii. Step 2: \"`lick`: cumulative lick count per frame (0-6)\".\n\n## 9-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick*?\n\ni. Two steps. First the paper's stuck-sensor correction: for each trial, if more than 35 % of frames have cumulative lick > 2, the whole trial's lick trace is set to 0 (the paper's `correct_lick_sensor_error` sets it to NaN; the AI uses 0 because the output must be a valid category). Then binarisation: any remaining value > 0 becomes 1. This affects 69 of 12,216 trials (0.6 %).\n\nii.\n```python\nLICK_ERROR_THR = 0.35  # fraction of frames with cumsum lick > 2\n...\n    lick_corrected = lick.copy()\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s = trial_start_inds[i] - 1; e = teleport_inds[i] - 1\n        trial_lick = lick_corrected[s:e]\n        n_frames = len(trial_lick)\n        if n_frames > 0 and np.sum(trial_lick > 2) / n_frames > LICK_ERROR_THR:\n            lick_corrected[s:e] = 0\n    lick_binary = (lick_corrected > 0).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. Step 1 identifies `correct_lick_sensor_error()` in `behavior.py` as a CURATION function (\"trials where >35% of frames have cumulative lick > 2 are set to NaN\"), and Step 5 Key Decision 10 states the intent: \"Following reference, if >35% of frames in a trial have cumulative lick count > 2, set lick to 0 for that trial (matching reference behavior.py).\" Binarisation follows the spec \"Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\".\n\n## 9-c. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward outcome*?\n\ni. For each trial the behavioural timestamps of the first and last sample of the trial define a time window `[t_start, t_end]`; the trial is scored 1 if any reward timestamp falls inside it, else 0. The binary value is broadcast constant over the trial (row 5). Resulting rate: 84.3 % rewarded / 15.7 % omitted.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef determine_reward_per_trial(reward_timestamps, behav_timestamps, trial_starts, teleports):\n    rewarded = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s = trial_starts[i] - 1\n        e = teleports[i] - 1\n        t_start = behav_timestamps[s]\n        t_end   = behav_timestamps[min(e, len(behav_timestamps) - 1)]\n        in_trial = (reward_timestamps >= t_start) & (reward_timestamps <= t_end)\n        if np.any(in_trial):\n            rewarded[i] = 1\n    return rewarded\n```\n```python\n            np.full((1, n_tp), reward_out, dtype=np.int64),   # (1, n_tp) - reward outcome\n```\n\niii. Working in timestamp space rather than index space avoids having to map the sparse reward events onto frames.\n\n## 12. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. The handled cases are: (a) a mismatch between the number of `trial_start` and `teleport` events — both lists are silently truncated to the shorter one; (b) degenerate trials with `teleport <= trial_start` — skipped; (c) sessions yielding fewer than 2 trials — skipped with a warning; (d) trials with no `reward_zone > 0` sample — previous trial's zone carried forward, zone A if it is the session's first trial; (e) `environment == -1` samples — excluded from the per-trial median, previous trial carried forward if no valid sample; (f) stuck lick sensor — trial's lick zeroed; (g) NaNs in the neural trace — replaced by 0 in the emitted trial matrices and by 0 before OASIS. A `min(e, len-1)` guard protects the reward-window lookup. There are *no* checks that the ophys and behaviour streams have the same number of samples (10 sessions, all m17/m18, have one extra imaging frame; harmless here because all indices come from the behaviour stream) and no assertion that reward timestamps land within one bin of a behavioural sample.\n\nii.\n```python\n    if len(trial_start_inds) != len(teleport_inds):\n        min_len = min(len(trial_start_inds), len(teleport_inds))\n        trial_start_inds = trial_start_inds[:min_len]\n        teleport_inds = teleport_inds[:min_len]\n```\n```python\n        if e <= s:\n            continue\n        ...\n        trial_neural[np.isnan(trial_neural)] = 0\n```\n```python\n        if len(neural_trials) < 2:\n            print(f\"    WARNING: Skipping session with {len(neural_trials)} trials\")\n            continue\n```\n```python\n        valid_env = env_vals[env_vals >= 0]\n        if len(valid_env) > 0:\n            env_per_trial[i] = int(np.median(valid_env))\n        elif i > 0:\n            env_per_trial[i] = env_per_trial[i - 1]\n```\n\niii. Undocumented by the AI. The discretisation helpers are already fully vectorised over a trial (boolean masks rather than per-sample loops), and there is no Viterbi/HMM pass. What remains are Python loops over trials: four separate trial loops inside `compute_dff_and_deconvolve` (masking, baseline, smoothing+deconvolution), and four more at session level (`determine_reward_zone_per_trial`, `determine_reward_per_trial`, the environment loop, the lick-correction loop) plus the main assembly loop — eight or nine passes over the same trial boundaries that could be merged into one, or expressed with `np.add.reduceat` / segment-wise operations. `determine_reward_per_trial` could be replaced by a single `np.searchsorted` of reward times against trial-start times.\n\nii.\n```python\n    for i in range(n_trials):   # reward zone\n    ...\n    for i in range(n_trials):   # autoreward\n    ...\n    for i in range(n_trials):   # environment\n    ...\n    for i in range(n_trials):   # lick correction\n    ...\n    for i in range(n_trials):   # main assembly\n```\n\niii. No rationale is given; the trial loops are inherent to the reference `dff()` implementation the AI copied (baseline and deconvolution are genuinely per-trial), and the session-level loops were left as written because the conversion already met the runtime budget.\n\n## 13-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. Undocumented by the AI. The script makes a single pass over each NWB file (no survey pass), so no file is read twice. Within a session, the trial-boundary index arithmetic `s = trial_start_inds[i] - 1; e = teleport_inds[i] - 1` is recomputed in eight different loops, and the trial windows are traversed three times inside `compute_dff_and_deconvolve` alone. `behav_timestamps` is read but the timing information it carries is recomputed independently as `arange(n_tp)/effective_rate`.\n\nii.\n```python\n    for start, stop in zip(start_inds, stop_inds):   # mask F/Fneu\n    ...\n    for start, stop in zip(start_inds, stop_inds):   # neuropil add-back + baseline\n    ...\n    for start, stop in zip(start_inds, stop_inds):   # smooth + deconvolve\n```\n\niii. No rationale recorded. The three-pass structure inside the dF/F function is inherited verbatim from the paper's `preprocessing.dff`, which the AI chose to reproduce faithfully (Step 5, Key Decision 1).\n\n## 13-d. A trial is the half-open window from a `trial_start` event to the next `teleport` event, i.e. the lap, excluding the inter-trial teleport period. The AI takes *every* sample where `trial_start > 0` and every sample where `teleport > 0`, converts them to 1-based indices, and truncates both lists to a common length if they differ. Per trial it then slices `[s, e)` with `s = trial_start-1` (the trial-start sample) and `e = teleport-1` (the teleport sample, exclusive).\n\nii.\n```python\n    trial_start_inds = np.where(trial_start_signal > 0)[0] + 1   # convert to 1-indexed\n    teleport_inds    = np.where(teleport_signal   > 0)[0] + 1\n\n    if len(trial_start_inds) != len(teleport_inds):\n        min_len = min(len(trial_start_inds), len(teleport_inds))\n        trial_start_inds = trial_start_inds[:min_len]\n        teleport_inds    = teleport_inds[:min_len]\n```\n```python\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s = trial_start_inds[i] - 1   # 0-indexed start\n        e = teleport_inds[i] - 1      # 0-indexed end\n        if e <= s:\n            continue\n```\n\niii. Step 5, Key Decision 5: \"Trial definition: Each trial runs from trial_start to teleport. Teleport/ITI periods excluded (set to NaN in reference).\" The 1-based convention was adopted so the same index arrays could be handed to the reference `dff()` function, which internally slices `start-1 : stop-1`. Step 10 checks trials/session range 41–100 and mean 80.4 against the paper's 80.5 ± 7.4.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Essentially no trial-level quality filter is applied. Only degenerate trials (`teleport_index <= trial_start_index`) are skipped, and sessions with <2 usable trials are dropped. There is no minimum-duration criterion. The one quality correction that *is* applied is per-trial and affects only the lick output, not trial inclusion: trials where >35 % of frames have cumulative lick > 2 are treated as a stuck lick sensor and have their lick trace zeroed (see 9-b). All 12,216 trials in the dataset are retained.\n\nii.\n```python\n        if e <= s:\n            continue\n```\n```python\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s = trial_start_inds[i] - 1\n        e = teleport_inds[i] - 1\n        trial_lick = lick_corrected[s:e]\n        n_frames = len(trial_lick)\n        if n_frames > 0 and np.sum(trial_lick > 2) / n_frames > LICK_ERROR_THR:\n            lick_corrected[s:e] = 0\n```\n\niii. Step 3, \"Trial curation rules\": \"1. Lick sensor error: trials with >35% of frames having cumulative lick > 2 -> lick data set to NaN for that trial. 2. All valid trials (trial_number >= 0) included.\" The AI found no other trial-exclusion rule in the paper, and Step 10 notes that the long trials it saw (up to 216 s) are \"genuine behavioral variability\" rather than artefacts.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. From the raw suite2p traces `processing/ophys/Fluorescence/plane<N>/data` (F) and `processing/ophys/Neuropil/plane<N>/data` (Fneu), pooled over planes. The NWB `Deconvolved` array is explicitly **not** used. Cell identity comes from `ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell`, with `planeIdx` used to map ROI rows to planes.\n\nii.\n```python\n        for plane in planes:\n            F_plane    = ophys[f'Fluorescence/{plane}/data'][:]\n            Fneu_plane = ophys[f'Neuropil/{plane}/data'][:]\n            pnum = int(plane.replace('plane', ''))\n            plane_roi_mask = plane_idx == pnum\n            iscell_plane = iscell_full[plane_roi_mask, 0]\n            assert F_plane.shape[1] == np.sum(plane_roi_mask), ...\n            F_all.append(F_plane); Fneu_all.append(Fneu_plane)\n            cell_mask_all.append(iscell_plane > 0)\n        F_concat    = np.concatenate(F_all, axis=1)\n        Fneu_concat = np.concatenate(Fneu_all, axis=1)\n        cell_mask   = np.concatenate(cell_mask_all)\n```\n\niii. Step 4, Discrepancies table: \"NWB Deconvolved = Raw suite2p deconv (no NaN in ITI) … Paper says Custom dF/F + OASIS → **Must recompute dF/F from F and Fneu**\". Step 5, Key Decision 1 repeats this. Key Decision 6: \"Multi-plane animals (m17, m18): Pool neurons from both planes (matching reference).\"\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. The AI re-implements the paper's `preprocessing.dff` in `compute_dff_and_deconvolve`: (1) blank everything outside laps to NaN, (2) subtract `0.7 * Fneu`, (3) per trial add back `0.7 *` the trial's mean neuropil, (4) per trial compute a maximin baseline (Gaussian σ = 15 samples along time, then a 300-sample running minimum, then a 300-sample running maximum ≈ the Methods' 20 s window), (5) `dF/F = (F − baseline) / |baseline|`, (6) per trial smooth dF/F with a 2-sample Gaussian, (7) per trial deconvolve with suite2p's OASIS at `tau = 0.7` and `frame_rate / n_planes`. dF/F is computed for *all* ROIs and the `iscell` subset is taken afterwards. Baselines are always restricted to the lap — the paper's `keep_teleports` branch (per-animal, per-day, from `teleport_metadata.py`) is never used.\n\nii.\n```python\n    f_ -= NEU_COEF * f_neu_\n    ...\n        f_[:, s:e] = f_[:, s:e] + NEU_COEF * np.nanmean(f_neu_[:, s:e], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n        flow[:, s:e] = nansmooth(f_[:, s:e], 15, axis=1)\n        flow[:, s:e] = minimum_filter1d(flow[:, s:e], BASELINE_WINDOW, axis=-1)\n        flow[:, s:e] = maximum_filter1d(flow[:, s:e], BASELINE_WINDOW, axis=-1)\n    dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n    ...\n        dff[:, s:e] = nansmooth(dff[:, s:e], DFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA, axis=1)\n        trial_dff = dff[:, s:e].copy()\n        trial_dff[np.isnan(trial_dff)] = 0\n        spks[:, s:e] = oasis(trial_dff, 2000, TAU, frame_rate / n_planes)\n```\n```python\nNEU_COEF = 0.7; TAU = 0.7; BASELINE_WINDOW = 300; DFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA = 2\n```\n\niii. Step 1 of the notes transcribes the eight-step pipeline out of `preprocessing.py` and Step 5 Key Decision 1 justifies recomputing it rather than using the stored `Deconvolved` array. The trajectory (step 45) records the AI explicitly rejecting the shortcut: \"the task explicitly says to match the reference processing, so I need to take that seriously rather than assume equivalence.\"\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Only one filter: ROIs with `iscell[:,0] > 0` (suite2p's curated cells) are kept. The paper's additional exclusion of putative interneurons (dF/F–speed Pearson r > 0.5) is deliberately **not** applied. Result: 138,678 neurons, 155–2,341 per session.\n\nii.\n```python\n            iscell_plane = iscell_full[plane_roi_mask, 0]\n            cell_mask_all.append(iscell_plane > 0)\n    ...\n    cell_indices = np.where(cell_mask)[0]\n    events_cells = events[cell_indices, :]   # (n_cells, n_timepoints)\n    n_neurons = len(cell_indices)\n```\n\niii. Step 5, Key Decision 3: \"We do NOT apply the interneuron correlation filter because: (a) it would require computing the full dF/F timeseries first and correlating with speed, (b) it only excludes ~0.42% of cells, (c) the iscell manual curation already removed obvious interneurons.\" Step 10 accounts for the resulting discrepancy: \"Max neuron count (2341, m18) slightly exceeds paper's 2172 because we skip interneuron correlation filter\". (Rationale (a) is not actually true of the AI's own script, which does compute the full dF/F trace.)\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Alignment to trial start requires no extra work: the first column of every trial's neural matrix is the frame at the `trial_start` index, because neural and behavioural samples share one frame index. Trial neural data is the slice `events_cells[:, s:e]`, NaNs replaced by 0 (there are none inside a lap by construction). `metadata['temporal_alignment_event'] = 'start of trial (trial_start signal)'`, `off_start = 0.0`, `off_end = None`.\n\nii.\n```python\n        trial_neural = events_cells[:, s:e].copy()\n        trial_neural[np.isnan(trial_neural)] = 0\n        neural_trials.append(trial_neural.astype(np.float32))\n```\n```python\n            'temporal_alignment_event': 'start of trial (trial_start signal)',\n            'off_start': 0.0,\n            'off_end': None,\n```\n\niii. Step 5, Key Decision 4/5 and the mapping table: neural and behaviour are sampled on the same imaging frames, so the trial window index is shared. The `--show-processing` figure plots the trial-0 neural trace against the behavioural traces on a common time axis as the visual check.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. One time bin = one imaging frame per plane ≈ 64.484 ms (15.5078125 Hz). No rebinning, resampling or interpolation is performed anywhere. For the two-plane animals (m17, m18) the stored `imaging_rate` is the 31.0156 Hz scanner rate, and the AI divides it by `n_planes` to recover the 15.5 Hz per-plane rate — used both for the OASIS kernel and for the time axis. `metadata['time_bin_size']` is hard-coded as `1000/15.5078125`.\n\nii.\n```python\n    effective_rate = imaging_rate\n    if n_planes > 1:\n        effective_rate = imaging_rate / n_planes\n    ...\n        spks[:, s:e] = oasis(trial_dff, 2000, TAU, frame_rate / n_planes)\n```\n```python\n            'time_bin_size': 1000.0 / 15.5078125,  # ms per frame\n            'imaging_rate_hz': 15.5078125,\n```\n\niii. Step 5, Key Decision 4: \"Time bin = imaging frame. Each time bin is one imaging frame (~64.5 ms at 15.5 Hz). This matches the reference temporal resolution.\" Step 2: \"Multi-plane animals (m17, m18) … Imaging rate ~31 Hz interleaved -> ~15.5 Hz per plane\", so bins are the same size for every session.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. Not from the stored behavioural `timestamps` at all. It is synthesised from the sample index within the trial and the per-plane imaging rate (`imaging_rate`, divided by `n_planes` where applicable). Behavioural `position/timestamps` is read, but only for the reward-event comparison.\n\nii.\n```python\n        time_from_start = np.arange(n_tp, dtype=np.float32) / effective_rate\n```\n\niii. The notes' Step 5 mapping table lists the transform as \"(frame_idx - trial_start_idx) / imaging_rate\". The implicit justification is that the acquisition clock is fixed: the AI treats frames as uniformly spaced at the imaging rate. (In this dataset that is exactly true — `np.diff(timestamps)` is constant to 1e-12 s and equals `n_planes/imaging_rate` — so the two routes give identical numbers.)\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds*?\n\ni. None beyond `index / rate`, which starts each trial at exactly 0.0 s and increases by one bin per sample. Stored as row 0 of the `(4, n_timepoints)` input array in float32. Full-data range: [0.0, 216.5] s.\n\nii.\n```python\n        time_from_start = np.arange(n_tp, dtype=np.float32) / effective_rate\n        input_tv = time_from_start.reshape(1, -1)\n        inputs = np.vstack([input_tv, ...]).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. No separate justification is given; it follows directly from the decoder-input spec \"Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\". Step 10 flags the 216.5 s maximum as worth checking and concludes it is a genuine long trial (m4/ses-04), not an alignment bug.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. By construction: the same `s:e` index window is used to slice the neural matrix and to generate the time vector, and both have length `n_tp = e - s`. The AI treats behavioural and ophys streams as sharing one frame index and performs no resampling, no offset correction, and no assertion that the two streams have equal length.\n\nii.\n```python\n        n_tp = e - s\n        trial_neural = events_cells[:, s:e].copy()\n        ...\n        time_from_start = np.arange(n_tp, dtype=np.float32) / effective_rate\n```\n\niii. Step 3, Processing Details: \"Temporal alignment: All data aligned to 2P imaging frames (~15.5 Hz)\". The `--show-processing` plots overlay neural traces and behavioural variables on a shared time axis to make misalignment visible; Step 7 records \"Processing plots generated and reviewed - all look correct.\"\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Environment type* derived from?\n\ni. The `environment` behavioural time series (`-1` before scanning starts, `0` = ENV1, `1` = ENV2).\n\nii.\n```python\n        environment = bts['environment/data'][:]\n```\n\niii. Step 2: \"`environment`: -1 before scanning, 0=ENV1, 1=ENV2\", reconciled in Step 4 with the paper's ENV1/ENV2 nomenclature.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Environment type*?\n\ni. Per trial, the AI takes the median of the environment samples inside the trial after discarding any negative (pre-scanning) value, casts to int, and broadcasts that single value across all timepoints of the trial (row 1 of the input array). If a trial had no valid sample, the previous trial's value is carried forward.\n\nii.\n```python\n    env_per_trial = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=np.int64)\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s = trial_start_inds[i] - 1\n        e = teleport_inds[i] - 1\n        env_vals = environment[s:e]\n        valid_env = env_vals[env_vals >= 0]\n        if len(valid_env) > 0:\n            env_per_trial[i] = int(np.median(valid_env))\n        elif i > 0:\n            env_per_trial[i] = env_per_trial[i - 1]\n```\n```python\n            np.full((1, n_tp), env_type, dtype=np.float32),   # (1, n_tp) - env type\n```\n\niii. Step 4 resolution: \"Environment encoding … -1 before scanning … Use env value per trial (ignoring -1).\" The median over the trial is the AI's robustness device against stray samples. Per-trial constants are broadcast to a full time series because the format spec prefers `(d_input, n_timepoints)` arrays.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Trial number* derived from?\n\ni. From the position of the trial in the `trial_start`/`teleport` sequence — i.e. the Python loop counter `i` — not from the NWB `trial number` time series. (`trial number` is read into a local variable but never used.)\n\nii.\n```python\n        trial_number = np.float32(i)\n```\n```python\n        trial_num = bts['trial number/data'][:]   # loaded, never used\n```\n\niii. The Step 5 mapping table lists \"NWB trial number → `input[2]`: trial_number, 0-indexed trial number\", but the implementation uses the loop index. The two coincide because trials are enumerated in temporal order from the same `trial_start` events; no explicit justification for preferring the counter is recorded.\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Trial number*?\n\ni. None. The 0-based within-session index is cast to float32 and broadcast constant across the trial's timepoints (row 2). It restarts at 0 in every session and reaches 99 in the 100-trial sessions.\n\nii.\n```python\n            np.full((1, n_tp), trial_number, dtype=np.float32),  # (1, n_tp) - trial number\n```\n\niii. Follows the decoder spec \"Trial number (continuous, per trial)\". Verified indirectly by the reported input range `trial_number: [0.0, 99.0]`.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Previous trial outcome* derived from?\n\ni. From the same per-trial `rewarded` vector used for the reward-outcome output. That vector is built from the sparse `Reward` event time series (its own `timestamps`, compared against `position/timestamps`), and is additionally OR-ed with the `autoreward` channel inside the trial.\n\nii.\n```python\n    rewarded = determine_reward_per_trial(\n        reward_timestamps, behav_timestamps, trial_start_inds, teleport_inds)\n\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s = trial_start_inds[i] - 1\n        e = teleport_inds[i] - 1\n        if np.any(autoreward[s:e] > 0):\n            rewarded[i] = 1\n```\n\niii. Step 2: \"`Reward`: separate array with reward event timestamps (sparse)\"; \"`autoreward`: binary, marks auto-reward delivery\". Step 3 notes \"Auto-reward on first 10 trials of new condition\", which is why the AI folds autoreward into the outcome. (In this release `autoreward` is identically 0 in all 152 sessions, so the OR is a no-op.)\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Previous trial outcome*?\n\ni. For trial *i* > 0 the value is `rewarded[i-1]` (1 if any reward event fell inside trial *i*−1, else 0). Trial 0 is set to 0. The value is broadcast constant over the trial (row 3).\n\nii.\n```python\n        if i == 0:\n            prev_outcome = np.float32(0)  # no previous trial\n        else:\n            prev_outcome = np.float32(rewarded[i - 1])\n        ...\n            np.full((1, n_tp), prev_outcome, dtype=np.float32),  # (1, n_tp) - prev outcome\n```\n\niii. Directly from the decoder spec \"Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\". The 0 for the first trial is the AI's convention for \"no previous trial\". Step 10 cross-checks the underlying reward rate at 84.7 % against the paper's ~15 % omission rate.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. From `position` together with a per-trial reward-zone assignment. The zone is identified from the `reward_zone` behavioural channel: within a trial the AI takes the mean of `position` over the samples where `reward_zone > 0` and assigns whichever of the fixed zones A = [80,130], B = [200,250], C = [320,370] has the nearest centre (105 / 225 / 345 cm). If a trial has no `reward_zone > 0` sample at all (≈16 % of trials), the previous trial's zone is carried forward; if it is the first trial of the session, zone A is assumed.\n\nii.\n```python\nREWARD_ZONES = {'A': (80, 130), 'B': (200, 250), 'C': (320, 370)}\nZONE_CENTERS = {k: (v[0] + v[1]) / 2 for k, v in REWARD_ZONES.items()}\n```\n```python\n        rz_active = trial_rz > 0\n        if np.any(rz_active):\n            rz_positions = trial_pos[rz_active]\n            rz_center = np.mean(rz_positions)\n        else:\n            if len(zone_labels) > 0:\n                zone_labels.append(zone_labels[-1]); zone_coords.append(zone_coords[-1]); continue\n            else:\n                zone_labels.append('A'); zone_coords.append(REWARD_ZONES['A']); continue\n        dists = {k: abs(rz_center - c) for k, c in ZONE_CENTERS.items()}\n        zone = min(dists, key=dists.get)\n```\n\niii. Step 1: \"Reward zone locations: A=[80,130], B=[200,250], C=[320,370] (from `reward_zone_dict` using X, Y, Z keys)\". Step 5 Key Decision 8: \"Reward zone determination per trial: Determine from position where reward_zone signal > 0. Zone A: center ~105 cm, Zone B: center ~225 cm, Zone C: center ~345 cm.\" The trajectory (step 38) shows the AI inspecting the raw `reward_zone` values (0–6) and concluding they are the VR zone signal rather than a distance measure. Step 10 validates the result: zone distribution 33.0 / 33.6 / 33.4 %.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. Signed distance in cm from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the assigned zone: negative before the zone, exactly 0 anywhere inside `[zone_start, zone_end]`, positive after.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, zone_start, zone_end):\n    dist = np.zeros_like(position)\n    before = position < zone_start\n    inside = (position >= zone_start) & (position <= zone_end)\n    after  = position > zone_end\n    dist[before] = position[before] - zone_start   # negative\n    dist[inside] = 0.0\n    dist[after]  = position[after] - zone_end      # positive\n    return dist\n```\n\niii. Step 5, Key Decision 7: \"Compute as signed distance from position to nearest point in the reward zone [start, end]. Negative = before zone, 0 = in zone, positive = past zone. Discretize per decoder spec.\" Step 10 sanity-checks the resulting distribution and explains the small bin-4 fraction (2.1 %) as the animals slowing/stopping in the zone.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Into the 7 categories of the decoder spec by explicit boolean masks rather than `np.digitize`: `<-50 → 0`, `[-50,-10) → 1`, `[-10,0) → 2`, `==0 → 3`, `(0,10] → 4`, `(10,50] → 5`, `>50 → 6`. Note the upper boundaries 10 and 50 are inclusive here (the spec's \"+10 to +50\" wording is ambiguous; this only matters for exactly-equal values).\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_distance_to_rz(distance):\n    bins = np.zeros(len(distance), dtype=np.int64)\n    bins[distance < -50] = 0\n    bins[(distance >= -50) & (distance < -10)] = 1\n    bins[(distance >= -10) & (distance < 0)] = 2\n    bins[distance == 0] = 3  # in zone\n    bins[(distance > 0) & (distance <= 10)] = 4\n    bins[(distance > 10) & (distance <= 50)] = 5\n    bins[distance > 50] = 6\n    return bins\n```\n\niii. Transcribed straight from the Decoder Outputs specification. `output_values` documents the labels; Step 7/10 confirm all 7 bins are populated and Step 10 discusses the asymmetry of the distribution.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. No alignment step. `position` is sliced with the same `s:e` window as the neural matrix, giving the same length and the same per-bin correspondence.\n\nii.\n```python\n        trial_pos = position[s:e]\n        zs, ze = zone_coords[i]\n        dist = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(trial_pos, zs, ze)\n        dist_binned = discretize_distance_to_rz(dist)\n```\n\niii. Same reasoning as 3-c: behaviour and ophys share the imaging frame index, so an identical slice is an identical alignment. The `--show-processing` figure plots the discretised distance for a sample trial as the visual check.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Absolute position* derived from?\n\ni. The `position` behavioural time series (cm along the VR corridor).\n\nii.\n```python\n        position = bts['position/data'][:]\n        ...\n        trial_pos = position[s:e]\n```\n\niii. Step 2: \"`position`: 0-450 cm on track, -500 in teleport zone, negative before track start\". Since trials stop at the teleport sample, the −500 teleport values never enter a trial.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n\ni. None beyond slicing and discretisation — the raw cm value is used as-is.\n\nii.\n```python\n        pos_binned = discretize_position(trial_pos)\n```\n\niii. The paper's 450 cm track and the spec's \"5 equal-sized bins spanning the 450 cm track\" mean the raw value needs no transformation.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Five 90 cm bins by boolean masks, with the first and last left open so that samples marginally outside [0, 450] fall into bins 0 and 4: `<90 → 0`, `[90,180) → 1`, `[180,270) → 2`, `[270,360) → 3`, `>=360 → 4`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_position(position):\n    bins = np.zeros(len(position), dtype=np.int64)\n    bins[position < 90] = 0\n    bins[(position >= 90) & (position < 180)] = 1\n    bins[(position >= 180) & (position < 270)] = 2\n    bins[(position >= 270) & (position < 360)] = 3\n    bins[position >= 360] = 4\n    return bins\n```\n\niii. Directly from the Decoder Outputs spec. Step 10 records \"Position bins: 5 populated / All populated — PASS\"; the converted fractions are 0.211 / 0.178 / 0.231 / 0.227 / 0.154.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Absolute position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. No alignment step; the same `s:e` trial slice is used as for the neural matrix.\n\nii.\n```python\n        trial_pos = position[s:e]\n        pos_binned = discretize_position(trial_pos)\n```\n\niii. As in 3-c/7-d: one shared frame index for behaviour and ophys.\n\n## 9-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick* derived from?\n\ni. The `lick` behavioural time series (cumulative lick count per frame, values 0–6).\n\nii.\n```python\n        lick = bts['lick/data'][:]\n```\n\niii. Step 2: \"`lick`: cumulative lick count per frame (0-6)\".\n\n## 9-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick*?\n\ni. Two steps. First the paper's stuck-sensor correction: for each trial, if more than 35 % of frames have cumulative lick > 2, the whole trial's lick trace is set to 0 (the paper's `correct_lick_sensor_error` sets it to NaN; the AI uses 0 because the output must be a valid category). Then binarisation: any remaining value > 0 becomes 1. This affects 69 of 12,216 trials (0.6 %).\n\nii.\n```python\nLICK_ERROR_THR = 0.35  # fraction of frames with cumsum lick > 2\n...\n    lick_corrected = lick.copy()\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s = trial_start_inds[i] - 1; e = teleport_inds[i] - 1\n        trial_lick = lick_corrected[s:e]\n        n_frames = len(trial_lick)\n        if n_frames > 0 and np.sum(trial_lick > 2) / n_frames > LICK_ERROR_THR:\n            lick_corrected[s:e] = 0\n    lick_binary = (lick_corrected > 0).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The AI re-implements the paper's `preprocessing.dff` in `compute_dff_and_deconvolve`: (1) blank everything outside laps to NaN, (2) subtract `0.7 * Fneu`, (3) per trial add back `0.7 *` the trial's mean neuropil, (4) per trial compute a maximin baseline (Gaussian σ = 15 samples along time, then a 300-sample running minimum, then a 300-sample running maximum ≈ the Methods' 20 s window), (5) `dF/F = (F − baseline) / |baseline|`, (6) per trial smooth dF/F with a 2-sample Gaussian, (7) per trial deconvolve with suite2p's OASIS at `tau = 0.7` and `frame_rate / n_planes`. dF/F is computed for *all* ROIs and the `iscell` subset is taken afterwards. Baselines are always restricted to the lap — the paper's `keep_teleports` branch (per-animal, per-day, from `teleport_metadata.py`) is never used.\n\nii.\n```python\n    f_ -= NEU_COEF * f_neu_\n    ...\n        f_[:, s:e] = f_[:, s:e] + NEU_COEF * np.nanmean(f_neu_[:, s:e], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n        flow[:, s:e] = nansmooth(f_[:, s:e], 15, axis=1)\n        flow[:, s:e] = minimum_filter1d(flow[:, s:e], BASELINE_WINDOW, axis=-1)\n        flow[:, s:e] = maximum_filter1d(flow[:, s:e], BASELINE_WINDOW, axis=-1)\n    dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n    ...\n        dff[:, s:e] = nansmooth(dff[:, s:e], DFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA, axis=1)\n        trial_dff = dff[:, s:e].copy()\n        trial_dff[np.isnan(trial_dff)] = 0\n        spks[:, s:e] = oasis(trial_dff, 2000, TAU, frame_rate / n_planes)\n```\n```python\nNEU_COEF = 0.7; TAU = 0.7; BASELINE_WINDOW = 300; DFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA = 2\n```\n\niii.
